I really pride myself on being polite and helpful at the store. So the only worry is just about letting my manager down or holding up a customer. I'm trying really hard to just be a good worker and fix it. Is that... does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself doing those little patterns or freezing for a moment, is there a thought in your mind that makes you feel like you have to do it, or does it feel more like something you just end up doing without really thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. There isn't a specific thought behind it, like feeling I absolutely have to do it or anything like that. It's really just... exactly like your second point, something I just end up doing without thinking. \n\nMy mind just sort of goes a little blank for a second, and my hand just does the tapping, or I just pause. I hope that doesn't make me sound too strange, or like I'm not paying attention to my job. I really do try my best to stay completely focused on the customers. I just sort of snap out of it and think, 'Whoops, back to work,' and move on. \n\nDid I explain that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Before you find yourself tapping the scanner or pausing, do you ever feel a sort of urge or odd sensation that comes just before you do it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. I don't feel any kind of... odd sensation or an urge building up or anything like that. It's just, well, like I mentioned, it just sort of happens out of the blue. \n\nI hope that's not frustrating for you to hear, since I know you're trying to put the pieces together. I really wish I did feel something beforehand, honestly, because then maybe I could catch myself and stop it before my manager notices! But no, there's really no warning or urge at all. It's just a little blank spot. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer... am I giving you the kind of details you need? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: While you’re in the middle of one of these episodes—like tapping the scanner or pausing—do you feel completely aware of what’s going on around you, or does everything seem a bit fuzzy or distant?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think it gets fuzzy or anything like that. I'm pretty sure I'm completely aware of everything going on. I can still hear the register beeping and the people talking in line, and the store music playing. \n\nI really wouldn't want you to think I'm completely checking out or ignoring my customers, because I really do try to stay alert. It's really just a brief little... physical pause, I guess you could say. But my ears and eyes are still working perfectly fine, and I'm right there the whole time. \n\nDoes that make sense? I want to make sure I'm explaining it clearly for your notes. I hope I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember everything that happens during one of these episodes afterward, like what the customer looked like or what they were buying, or do you ever feel confused about what just happened once it’s over?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I can usually remember everything just fine. I don't feel confused afterward, not really. I know exactly what the customer was buying, like if it was a gallon of milk or whatever. \n\nIt's just... well, it's just that I keep getting stuck doing these little things that don't quite fit the moment, you know? Like I said, tapping the scanner in a pattern, or... or sometimes I'll just freeze with my hand hovering right over the open cash drawer while the poor customer is just standing there waiting. \n\nI really hate to admit this, because I pride myself on being a good worker, but... well, last week, it actually got me written up by my manager. I was so embarrassed. I really don't want you to think I'm a bad employee, I just... I get stuck for a second. \n\nIs that the kind of detail you were looking for? I hope I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do these little patterns or pauses happen only at work, or do you ever notice them happening in other situations, like at home or when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, mostly just at work, I think. I mean, I really try to keep things perfectly normal when I'm at home or, you know, if I'm out with anyone. \n\nI guess... well, maybe once in a great while I might pause for a second while I'm making coffee or something, but it's really nothing major. Nothing that anyone else would even notice, I promise! I definitely don't sit around doing weird things at home. It's really just the stress of the checkout line, I think. \n\nI hope it's okay that it's mostly just a work thing... does that still fit with what you're looking into? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed yourself making small, sudden movements — like blinking your eyes, shrugging your shoulders, or making a sound — that you don’t plan to do and can’t always control, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I haven't noticed anything like that. I definitely don't make any sudden sounds or... or shrug uncontrollably or anything. \n\nI always try to be very polite and contained, you know? I wouldn't want to make anyone uncomfortable by making weird noises or twitching, so I'm pretty sure I'd notice if I was doing something like that. I really do try to stay in control of myself. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't have that... that particular thing you're asking about. I don't want you to think I'm holding anything back from you, I just really haven't experienced it. Am I still giving you enough information to go on? I just want to make sure I'm being a good help to you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed yourself making small, sudden movements — like blinking your eyes, shrugging your shoulders, or making a sound — that you don’t plan to do and can’t always control, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, I'm sorry, did I... did I not explain that well enough just a second ago? I just sort of snap out of it and think, 'Whoops, back to work,' and move on. \n\nDid I explain that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Before you find yourself tapping the scanner or pausing, do you ever feel a sort of urge or odd sensation that comes just before you do it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. I don't feel any kind of... odd sensation or an urge building up or anything like that. It's really just... exactly like your second point, something I just end up doing without thinking. \n\nMy mind just sort of goes a little blank for a second, and my hand just does the tapping, or I just pause. I just sort of snap out of it and think, 'Whoops, back to work,' and move on. \n\nDid I explain that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Before you find yourself tapping the scanner or pausing, do you ever feel a sort of urge or odd sensation that comes just before you do it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. I don't feel any kind of... odd sensation or an urge building up or anything like that. It's just, well, like I mentioned, it just sort of happens out of the blue. \n\nI hope that's not frustrating for you to hear, since I know you're trying to put the pieces together. It's just a little blank spot. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer... am I giving you the kind of details you need? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: While you’re in the middle of one of these episodes—like tapping the scanner or pausing—do you feel completely aware of what’s going on around you, or does everything seem a bit fuzzy or distant?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think it gets fuzzy or anything like that. I'm pretty sure I'm completely aware of everything going on.
